They actually do cut down on the drying time, which is nice because our dryer is on its last legs and makes this awful screeching noise if you put too many heavy towels in it at once. The repair guy said it would cost more to fix than to just buy a new one, which is just how everything is nowadays, isn't it? Nobody fixes anything, they just throw it in a landfill. \n\nBut anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, the mornings. Yeah, I wake up already feeling tired, and then by the time I've been at my desk for a couple of hours dealing with those claims, my brain is just totally running on empty again.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you feel about the things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with family and friends?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, I've really pulled back from a lot of things. I used to love painting, and I'd go hiking on the weekends, or volunteer... but lately, I'm just trying to get through the week. It's like I don't have the energy for any of it anymore. Even at work, I've started missing details in audits, and it's taking me way longer to sign off on files. \n\nWhich is a real problem because my supervisor, Gary, he's a stickler for turnaround times. He's been with the company for twenty-five years, and he still uses this ancient label maker for all his physical folders. You can hear it clicking away from three cubicles down. Click, click, click. It drives the new girl absolutely crazy. She actually asked me if we could requisition a digital one for him, but I told her, 'Honey, Gary is not giving up that label maker until he retires.' He’s got this whole system where red tape means urgent, yellow means pending... but he ran out of yellow tape last month and started using this neon green color, and it completely threw off the filing clerks. They were bringing him closed files thinking they were new assignments. It was a whole mess on Tuesday. \n\nBut yeah... what was the question? Oh, hobbies. Yeah, I just don't do much of anything anymore outside of work. I'm just too exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or hopeless most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I have. It's... most days I feel like there’s this gray film over everything. Like, even when my husband is being really sweet or we’re all in the kitchen cooking with the family, I'm just going through the motions. I don't really feel that spark or enjoyment I used to. \n\nWe were making lasagna last Sunday, actually. My mother's recipe. She always insisted on using half ground beef and half Italian sausage, which really does make a difference in the flavor. But you have to get the mild sausage, not the spicy, or it totally overpowers the ricotta. We used to get the sausage from this little deli down on 4th Street, but they closed during the pandemic and never reopened. It's a shame, they had the best cured meats. Now they're turning the building into another one of those boutique fitness places. I think it's a Pilates studio? My neighbor Sarah goes to one of those, and she says it's great for your core, but honestly, who wants to pay thirty dollars a class just to stretch on a fancy machine? \n\nSorry, I'm wandering again, aren't I? But yes, to answer your question, I just feel kind of down and disconnected most of the time, even when good things are happening around me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, like painting or hiking?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I think we just talked about that a minute ago, but yeah, I really have. I just don't have the energy for the painting or the hiking anymore. I haven't touched my canvases in months. \n\nActually, the last thing I tried to paint was a landscape from a photo I took at Mount Rainier. We went there for our anniversary a couple of years ago. It was so beautiful, but the traffic getting into the park was an absolute nightmare. We sat in a line of cars for over an hour just to get through the toll booth. And the guy in front of us had this bumper sticker that said 'My other ride is a UFO,' which I thought was kind of funny at first, but after staring at it for an hour, it just got annoying. We finally got in and did the Skyline Trail, which is where I took the photo. It was gorgeous, but my husband wore these new hiking boots he hadn't broken in yet, and he got the worst blisters. I told him he needed to wear them around the house first, but you know how men are, they never listen. He ended up having to buy these expensive moleskin pads at the visitor center... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I haven't painted that picture or any other picture in a long time. I just don't care to do it right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Have these feelings of fatigue, sadness, and loss of interest been going on for at least two weeks, or longer?\n\nPatient: Oh, two weeks? No, no, it's been way longer than that. I'd say it's been about... well, probably three years and three months now. Yeah, that sounds right. It started right around the time I was moved into this new role at work. They gave me a lot more responsibility, having me mentor the new adjusters and oversee the rollout of our new workflow systems. \n\nWhich, let me tell you, those new systems were a complete disaster when they first launched. The IT department swore up and down that the transition would be seamless, but the first week, nobody could access the old claim files. It was a nightmare. I had adjusters calling me in a panic because they had clients on the phone asking about their settlements, and we couldn't even pull up the policy numbers. And the interface! They changed all the color coding. We used to have blue for auto claims and green for property, but they switched it to this weird pastel palette that you can barely read on the monitors. I actually had to go out and buy a pair of blue-light glasses just to stare at the screen all day. I found a nice pair at Costco, actually, in a tortoiseshell frame. They came in a two-pack, so I gave the extra pair to my husband, but he keeps leaving them in his truck where they get all smudged up...\n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the timeline. Yeah, it's been over three years since I started feeling this way, ever since that promotion.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not good enough, or that you're letting people down at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I'm letting anyone down or that I'm not good enough. I just... I do my job, and I take care of the house, even if I'm just going through the motions. No, my weight has been pretty much exactly the same. I know for sure because I just had to buy some new slacks for work last weekend. Our office decided to enforce a strict business casual dress code again, which is honestly ridiculous because we're on a hybrid schedule now and half the time nobody even sees us in person anyway. \n\nBut I went down to Kohl's to get a couple of pairs of basic black pants. I usually get the ones with the little bit of stretch in the waistband, but they completely rearranged the women's department. I had to squeeze past the clearance rack just to get around her, and I ended up knocking a bunch of those silk scarves onto the floor. Yeah, no, I bought the exact same size twelve I've been wearing for the last five years and they fit just like always. Usually the snowbirds go south for the winter, not the other way around. Yeah, no, I think I already answered that. I haven't noticed anything else besides the work stuff I just mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you noticed that it's been harder for you to make decisions or focus on tasks, even ones that used to feel simple?\n\nPatient: I feel like we're going in circles here. They always had every brand except the one I needed. It's like they do it on purpose, right? Just to make you buy a whole new pen. \n\nBut, uh, no. To answer your question, I haven't ever felt like this before. This is definitely new for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting two weeks or more when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had little energy, before this current one started?\n\nPatient: Are you... didn't you just ask me that? Like, literally the exact same question just a minute ago? I feel like we're going in circles here. \n\nNo, like I just said, I've never had a period like this before. I've always been the steady one. Even when things get chaotic, I just put my head down and work. \n\nLike with my sister's wedding back in the nineties. Oh my gosh, what a nightmare that was. The caterer backed out two weeks before the date, and my mother was having an absolute meltdown. She was calling everyone in the tri-state area trying to find someone who could do a sit-down dinner for two hundred people on a Saturday in June. It was impossible. We ended up having to go with this buffet place that mostly did corporate lunches, and the food was just... well, it was edible, but barely. The chicken was so dry you needed a gallon of water just to swallow it. And don't even get me started on the DJ. He played the Macarena three times. Three times! Who wants to do the Macarena in a heavy satin bridesmaid dress? I was sweating through the fabric, and the color was this awful seafoam green that washes out my complexion anyway. I still have the dress in a garment bag in the attic. I don't know why I keep it. Maybe I thought my daughter would want to wear it for Halloween or something, but she's more into those scary costumes with the fake blood. \n\nAnyway... yeah. I already answered this. No, I haven't felt this way before. It's just been recently.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had an episode where you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Okay, is this a test? Are you testing my memory or something? Because you literally just asked me that. Twice now. I feel like I'm taking crazy pills here. \n\nNo. For the third time, no. I have never felt like this before. It's just this one long stretch that I'm in right now. I don't know how else to say it. \n\nI mean, if you want to talk about memory tests, my mother-in-law had to take one of those when she started getting forgetful. They made her draw a clock, which is so outdated if you think about it. Who even looks at analog clocks anymore? My husband bought one of those smart displays for the kitchen counter, and it just shows the time in big digital numbers, plus the weather. It's supposed to show our family photos as a screensaver, but he set it up wrong, so it just cycles through the stock photos that came with the device. There's this one picture of a golden retriever running through a field of lavender that pops up every five minutes. We don't even own a dog. We had a cat once, Mr. Whiskers, but he ruined the upholstery on my favorite armchair. It was this beautiful floral print wingback chair I found at an antique store in Vermont. We took a road trip up there for the fall foliage, and the leaves were just... spectacular. \n\nBut seriously, doctor. I've already answered this. No previous episodes. Just this current one. Can we please move on to the next question?\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had an episode where you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Are you serious right now? Are you reading from a broken script or something? Because this is the fourth time—the *fourth* time—you've asked me the exact same thing. I'm starting to wonder if I'm the one who should be asking the questions here. \n\nI don't... I just don't know what you want me to say that I haven't already said. No. The answer is still no. Never before this. Just now. Just this one time that's been going on forever. \n\nIt's like dealing with customer service at the cable company. You call them up because the internet keeps dropping out, and every time they transfer you to a new person, you have to explain the whole problem all over again. And they always ask, \"Have you tried unplugging the router?\" Yes, Kevin, I've unplugged the router. I unplugged it, I waited thirty seconds, I plugged it back in, and the little light is still blinking orange instead of green. It's supposed to be a solid green light. I know this because the installation guy, he was this young kid, maybe twenty, he showed me specifically. He had these huge gauge earrings, you know, the ones that stretch out your earlobes? I always wonder what happens to those when they get older. Do the holes just stay there, or do they shrink back? I saw a documentary once about indigenous tribes in the Amazon who do that, but it has a cultural significance for them. I doubt the kid installing my Wi-Fi was doing it for cultural reasons. Probably just trying to annoy his parents. \n\nLook, I'm sorry if I'm getting snappy, but I just... I really need you to listen to me. No previous times like this. Okay? Can we please, please talk about something else?"
